People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. It's not worry, exactly. Just... restlessness, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do certain things over and over?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. Not that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Impulse? Um... no, I don't think so. I just... I mean, I just go about my day. I don't really do things like that. Not that I can think of, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Talking? Um... no. I don't really think I do. I usually just... keep to myself, mostly. So, no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself engaging in more activities or taking on more tasks than others?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. I don't really do all that much these days anyway... just my normal routine, I guess. I don't take on extra things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget or feel restless when you're sitting with other people?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose so. I don't mean to. Just... like in church meetings, I guess. I might tap my foot a bit... or just rub the edge of my purse. I shift around in my chair sometimes. The ladies beside me usually start looking over... so, I suppose I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose I do. Like... if I'm trying to sort my bills at the kitchen table. It's just... if I hear a car door outside, or the phone lights up... or even if I just start thinking about the garden. I just... get completely pulled off track. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: Are your restlessness and difficulty sitting still interfering with your work or daily activities?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. I suppose so. My family... well, they've actually had to step in with the bills and paperwork lately. And... at church, and the garden group... I guess people have had to cover responsibilities I dropped, or redo plans because of me. It's... well, it's rather embarrassing to admit. I just... I don't think the groups I care about feel they can count on me anymore.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start feeling this restlessness and inability to sit still?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's really been a problem for... about a year and ten months now. Since... since my husband passed away. That's when all this really started causing trouble.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble following through on instructions or finishing tasks because you lose focus?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose that happens. Um... like with the quilting charity. If they give me three or four things to do... I might start the first one right away. But then... well, I just sort of wander off into another chore. And... the last steps just never get done. So... yes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I suppose that happens. Um... like with the quilting charity. If they give me three or four things to do... I might start the first one right away. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're talking?\n\nPatient: I... yes. More than I mean to, I suppose. Um... especially during the committee meetings. A thought will just... pop into my head, and I end up blurting it out. Even if someone else is still talking. I just... I know it's rude, but if I don't say it right then, I'll lose it. So... yes, I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty sustaining focus during tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: I... yes, I suppose. Um... like if I sit down to read the senior center newsletter. I don't really do things like that. Not that I can think of, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Talking? Um... no. I don't really think I do. I usually just... keep to myself, mostly. So, no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself engaging in more activities or taking on more tasks than others?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. I don't really do all that much these days anyway... just my normal routine, I guess. Just... like in church meetings, I guess. I might tap my foot a bit... or just rub the edge of my purse. I shift around in my chair sometimes. Like... if I'm trying to sort my bills at the kitchen table. It's just... if I hear a car door outside, or the phone lights up... or even if I just start thinking about the garden. I just... get completely pulled off track. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: Are your restlessness and difficulty sitting still interfering with your work or daily activities?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. I suppose so. My family... well, they've actually had to step in with the bills and paperwork lately. And... at church, and the garden group... But then... well, I just sort of wander off into another chore. And... the last steps just never get done. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're talking?\n\nPatient: I... yes. More than I mean to, I suppose. Um... especially during the committee meetings. So... yes, I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty sustaining focus during tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: I... yes, I suppose. Um... like if I sit down to read the senior center newsletter. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often make careless mistakes when doing things like chores, work tasks, or activities?\n\nPatient: I... yes. I suppose so. Just... little mistakes, mostly. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when you first started feeling this restlessness and inability to sit still?\n\nPatient: I... well, I don't know an exact age. Just... always, I suppose. \n\nUm... even back in grade school. My teachers... they used to write that I was bright, but careless. I was always losing my papers, or... talking out of turn. They had to keep giving me reminders to... to stay in my seat. Or to just finish my work. So... it's been a very long time.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with restlessness, fidgeting, or finishing tasks when you were a child?\n\nPatient: I... well, I thought I just mentioned that. Um... yes. The teachers always said I was... out of my seat. And not finishing my work. So... yes, it was a problem back then, too.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I suppose so. My family... well, they've actually had to step in with the bills and paperwork lately. And... at church, and the garden group... I guess people have had to cover responsibilities I dropped, or redo plans because of me. It's... well, it's rather embarrassing to admit. I just... I don't think the groups I care about feel they can count on me anymore.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start feeling this restlessness and inability to sit still?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's really been a problem for... about a year and ten months now. Since... since my husband passed away. That's when all this really started causing trouble.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble following through on instructions or finishing tasks because you lose focus?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose that happens. Um... like with the quilting charity. If they give me three or four things to do... I might start the first one right away. But then... well, I just sort of wander off into another chore. And... the last steps just never get done. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're talking?\n\nPatient: I... yes. More than I mean to, I suppose. Um... especially during the committee meetings. A thought will just... pop into my head, and I end up blurting it out. Even if someone else is still talking. I just... I know it's rude, but if I don't say it right then, I'll lose it. So... yes, I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty sustaining focus during tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: I... yes, I suppose. Um... like if I sit down to read the senior center newsletter. Or... maybe the choir schedule. I start out fine, but... well, after a few minutes, I'll realize my eyes have just gone over the same lines again and again. And I... I have no idea what I just read. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often make careless mistakes when doing things like chores, work tasks, or activities?\n\nPatient: I... yes. I suppose so. Just... little mistakes, mostly. But they... well, they end up turning into big messes. \n\nUm... like with the garden club checkbook. I'll write down the wrong amount. Or... the church supper list. I'll leave a name off, even when I... I really thought I checked it. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when you first started feeling this restlessness and inability to sit still?\n\nPatient: I... well, I don't know an exact age. Just... always, I suppose. \n\nUm... even back in grade school. My teachers... they used to write that I was bright, but careless. I was always losing my papers, or... talking out of turn. They had to keep giving me reminders to... to stay in my seat. Or to just finish my work. So... it's been a very long time.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with restlessness, fidgeting, or finishing tasks when you were a child?\n\nPatient: I... well, I thought I just mentioned that. Um... yes. The teachers always said I was... out of my seat. And not finishing my work. So... yes, it was a problem back then, too.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these problems with restlessness and finishing tasks in multiple areas of your life, such as at home, in social situations, and in other activities?\n\nPatient: Yes. I... I suppose it affects everything. \n\nUm... at home, my family has had to... well, step in. With the bills and the paperwork. And... I mentioned the church and the garden club earlier. The people there... they've had to redo plans. Or cover responsibilities I just... dropped. \n\nIt's... I don't really like talking about it. I feel very embarrassed. It just seems like... the groups I care about, they don't feel they can count on me anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
